what can I type in the filter?
anything on the card — a team, a league, a country, a code, a rung (o2.5), a mark (hit, miss, push, open).
lane kinds — team over, match under (also ft under), double chance, draw no bet, result lane, playable. Tie one to a lane with tip 1 under, tip 2 over.
an absent lane — tip 2 none (also no tip 3): the cards where that lane never printed.
a probability threshold — tip 2 <80, tip 1 80>, tip 3 <=70, or prob <60 for the card's headline number. The arrow points at the side it keeps and can sit either way round; the % is optional.
a goal threshold — goal >3, goal <3: how the match actually finished, on settled cards only.
commas narrow — brazil, serie b, tip 1 under, 80> is all four at once.
Only what the guard would actually stake: the starred lane cleared its label's break-even by 6% and the tier is not red. ★ STRONG adds a top-quartile confluence score in Europe — on 1,008 replayed bets those graded 81.0% and +8.87%, against 72.8% and −0.67% for the rest.

🎓 Learn Athena — how to read a block

Real Madrid v Real Sociedadmore ▾
🕑 26-08 21:00 · LaLiga (81.7 +3.4)
🧠 elite attack vs leaky defence
Tip 1 O1.5 81.2% +6.8%
buy≥1.32 (+8.0% margin)
Tip 2 Real Madrid O1.5 67.0% +24.7% (team)
buy≥1.43 (−4.2% margin)
Real Madrid: elite attack, elite defence, in form — against Real Sociedad: leaky defence, struggling. That pairing — firepower against a defence that leaks — is where the goals in this tip come from.
The matchuphome team first, away team second — venue matters and is already in the numbers.
🕑 / 🔴 / ✅❌the clock before kickoff, red while live, then the verdict with the final score once graded.
The league linethe competition. On cup cards, · probationary marks a lane still earning trust with live results.
(81.7 +3.4)the league's proven PLAYABLE record: how its above-bar lanes — the ones worth betting — actually land, and how far that sits from the league's all-tips baseline. A consensus-cap league shows (82.0 capped): no lane there may claim edge, so it has no playable record to show.
🧠 the readwhat Athena measured in this matchup, in keywords — tap the card for the full story in sentences.
Tip 1the engine's best market. O1.5 = over 1.5 goals in the match; 81.2% = claimed probability; +6.8% = edge over a typical match.
buy≥1.32 (+8.0% margin)the minimum odds that make this tip worth money. The price listens to the tip AND the league's playable record — a tip below that record leans on it (0.4/0.6), one above it mostly trusts itself (0.8/0.2) — so lower-probability lanes become reachable at real-world odds. The bracket is what margin the printed price still holds over the tip's own break-even: negative means the blend is reaching down to make the lane buyable, eyes open.
Green border / lanea playable lane: edge above +1%. These are the tips with real value — the rest are shown for honesty, not for money.
Tip 2 · more ▾the second lane. (team) means a TEAM total — here Real Madrid alone to score 2+ — a different market from the match total, offered when its edge beats the ladder's runner-up. On other cards (floor −x) says how far Tip 2 sits below the confidence bar.
🧠 the storytap any card open: the read in full sentences — every phrase maps to something measured (attack and defence bands, form, table stakes, cup Elo), never invented.

Patches & calibrations of this era

  • Cups reopened on the probationary Club Elo lane and graded 12/16 on their first playoff night
  • Rules 5 and 6 became numbers; the board became this app
  • The calibration day: the retrosim page forced every number to be defended — HIGH_SAYS_DEBIT, per-league floors under an ROI constraint, board-wide 82.4 → 83.7
  • Closed at 81.9% tips / −7.5% ROI: the gap between hitrate and price is the lesson Session #4 inherits

Patches & calibrations of this era

Retrosim confirmed hitrates

Hit grades every Tip 1 the league produced, filter or no filter; Playable hit is the same replay narrowed to the lanes the board actually offers (edge above +1%) — the number the Playable tab lives on, with that subset's tip count in brackets. This very column exposed seven leagues whose above-bar lanes ran a flat 6–7 points hot in both half-windows while their consensus lanes underclaimed: there the published probability is now capped at the league consensus, so no lane can claim edge and none can badge playable.

Every league's Tip 1, replayed as-of on the current build over its most recent fixtures — up to 800, capped at two seasons. hit is what landed; gap is hit minus what the engine claimed — near zero means the engine tells the truth about itself, and at this sample size a row inside ±3 is within noise of honest. All published debits included. Click a column to sort — again to reverse.

What this table is and is not: a backtest on the real as-of code path — nothing dated after a match is read when pricing it — but the engine's constants were tuned on windows that overlap these fixtures, so read it as the engine describing itself, not as out-of-sample proof. The number that carries no such caveat is the confirmed hitrate on the Home page, measured only on tips published before kickoff. A push or half-win counts as a hit, a half-loss as a miss — the same rule the stated probability is computed with, so the gap compares like with like.

Patchlist & notes

What changed and why, one line each. The full evidence behind every line lives in the repository's README and scripts.

DateAreaChange
2026-08-31interfaceBUG: Ask Athena's filter matched nothing on lane words — 'team over' and 'tip 2' both returned 0 of 713. Cause was an escaping trap: the page is built from one big Python f-string, and a JS regex written as \\b there is parsed by PYTHON first, where \\b is the backspace character. The rendered regex therefore demanded an invisible control character before the O or U and never matched, so no over/under, team or tip-2 tokens ever entered a bank card's haystack — while the result-lane tokens, whose regex has no escapes, worked fine and hid the breakage. Both regexes now use (?:^|[^A-Za-z]) instead of a word boundary, which needs no backslash at all. Verified in node against the real Premier League bank: 'team over' 271 rows, 'tip 1 under' 387 at 87.1%, 'draw no bet' 230 at 82.2% on tip 3
boardBUG: this session's fixtures were counted TWICE in the bank. Merging the board's own results into the results store (31 Aug) meant the matchbank rebuild replayed them as retro rows, while webapp still appended the live board copy — 288 duplicated fixtures. The hero window, which takes the 300 most recent graded playable lanes, was therefore built from a doubled weekend and read 78.0% instead of 80.7%. The board rows are now dropped where a retro row already covers the fixture (the retro row wins: it carries all three graded lanes), and the hero computation moved after that pass, since it had been running before it. The bettor spotted the drop and asked whether the retrosim caused it — it did, through the ingest, but the honest number was never as low as the page said
interfaceA league on its own is now a valid Ask Athena query: it returns everything the bank holds for that competition, newest first — 821 rows for the Championship, 713 for the Premier League — and the filter bar below cuts it down, which is the point (the bettor: search a league, then filter team over). A date still narrows within a league as before. Bank cards became compact and expandable: the summary carries the score, the date and a chip per graded lane, and 'more' opens the three cells in full, so a several-hundred-row answer reads as a scoreboard rather than a wall. Head-to-head answers and short lists open by default
interfaceThe match bank now carries and grades ALL THREE lanes on every one of its 30,275 fixtures — the bettor asked for it so the bank could be cross-examined the way the board can. matchbank.py runs each fixture through the same two_tips path a live card uses and stores tip 1, tip 2 and tip 3 with their own marks, formatted by the board's own cell1/cell2/cell3 so the text is identical. Coverage: tip 1 on 30,220, tip 2 on 22,503 (74%), tip 3 on 12,722 (42%) — the lower two only print above their own bars, which is the honest number, not a gap. Ask Athena's results gain the fourth counter and the result-lane vocabulary. Bank-wide the three read 83.8 / 69.7 / 78.9, within a point of the 300-window baselines computed independently
interfaceAsk Athena's results get the same filter and a counter. A bank card now carries the board's search vocabulary — teams, competition, country, date, lane kind, and its own mark — so a league-and-date lookup can be narrowed further and scored: 'tip 1 under' inside the Premier League bank returns 389 matches at 87.1%, 'tip 1 over' 321 at 79.4%. Two counters rather than four, because the bank grades ONE lane: retrosim rows carry tip 1's mark and nothing else, tip 2 only where a board row supplied it, and tip 3 is not in the bank at all (it postdates the retrosim). The counter says 'tip 1 · bank' so it cannot be mistaken for the board's four
interfaceFound bets joins the filter: a bet row now carries its fixture's league, code and country plus its own lane kind (team over, double chance, draw no bet, match under) and its mark, so 'netherlands' returns the Eredivisie and Eerste Divisie positions together and 'team over' returns every team over taken. The counter row becomes tab-aware — four lane records on Completed, ONE taken-bets record on Found bets, nothing on the two pending tabs where there is nothing to score. Unfiltered the bets counter reproduces the tile exactly (104/130). First readings worth keeping: double chances 88.0% on 25, draw-no-bets 64.3% on 14
interfaceThe filter learns countries, folds accents, and reserves exact matching for collisions a country word cannot break. config/countries.tsv maps a league code's prefix to its country (typed like the nickname file — culture, not data, and the engine never reads it), so 'spain', 'brazil', 'japan' work. Every haystack also carries an accent-free copy and the query is folded, so 'serie b' finds 'Série B' and 'brasileirao' finds 'Brasileirão'. Exact matching now fires only where the colliding names share a country — laliga inside laliga 2, bundesliga inside 2. bundesliga, brasileirao inside brasileirao serie b — because there no country term can separate them. Italy's Serie B inside Brazil's stays a substring, so 'brazil, serie b' (12) and 'italy, serie b' (10) both resolve, exactly as the bettor asked
2026-08-30interfaceThe filter learns lane KINDS and gains comma-AND. A card's haystack now carries what each lane is, not just the fixture's names: team over, team under, match over, match under, double chance, draw no bet, result lane, playable, capped, and the settled mark (hit/miss/push). Commas narrow rather than widen — 'real madrid, team over' is the Madrid card that also offers a team over — and the live counters recount over whatever survives. Board-wide the vocabulary resolves: 109 team overs, 26 team unders, 76 draw-no-bets, 60 double chances
interfaceThe filter bar becomes an analysis tool: four counters under the search box recount final pick / tip 1 / tip 2 / tip 3 over whatever the filter leaves on screen — a league, a club, a rung, anything the haystack matches — and show the whole run when the box is empty. Every settled card carries its own four grades as data attributes (tip 1 and tip 2 at the playable standard, tip 3 whole, the star's own lane for the final pick), so the page counts marks rather than re-deriving anything from text. Verified against the tiles: unfiltered, the counters read 80.3 / 79.5 / 67.4 / 78.2 — the same four numbers
ledgerThe Prob column goes universal: scripts/lane_price.py prices ANY lane on a boarded fixture, not just the ones the card printed. The bettor asked for a number on their own reads — the one part of the book that had none, and the part where a number is most useful. Three engine figures per fixture (goal expectation, each side's chance to score) are enough to price the whole lane space: ladder rungs through p_win, team rungs through the same curves on one side's gf, result lanes through the tilted Poisson pair behind Tip 3. Computed once per fixture and cached in config/lane_cache.json (derived — delete it and the next render rebuilds it), so the table gains a probability for every row without a per-bet engine call. Verified against the cards: a lane the card DID print reprices to the same number. Dashes now mean only one thing — the engine abstains on that fixture. 144 bets, 0 dashes
engineBUG: the cross-division fallback for promoted clubs has been dead. Scaling an adjacent division's goals writes fractional values into fixtures.tsv's int64 goal columns, and pandas raises rather than upcasting — so every club whose history lives one division down crashed the whole request and the card printed an abstention. Found when Lincoln v Blackburn failed to price (Athletico Paranaense had failed the same way earlier and was worked around by renaming, which was the wrong diagnosis). Goals are cast to float before scaling; Lincoln and Athletico both price now. The fallback was shipped and validated back in the era when pandas upcast silently — a reminder that a dependency can retire a feature without anyone editing it
rulesRule 7 gains its second clause, from the day's worst pocket: SKIP tip 2 lanes and skip the Rule 6 HARDER-line translations. The Norwegian 17:00 wall took three tickets of exactly those shapes and lost all three (Kristiansund U1.5 team lane 1.41, IK Start U3.5 harder 1.41, Tromsø O2.5 harder 1.50), and Randers' team over — bought at 1.83, above its ask — was cashed out at 0.63x rather than lost whole. This is not one bad evening read as law: the final-picking replay already measured tip 2 lanes 12.7 points below tip 1 on 16,554 fixtures, and the star was restricted to tip 1 or tip 3 for the same reason this morning. The ledger now matches the card. Ledger also gains partial cash-outs: column 5 holds the return multiple rather than a flag, so 0.63 books what actually came back
boardThe baseline bar leads with the FINAL PICK — the card's starred lane replayed over each league's last 300 matches (82.1%), in front of the per-tip baselines — and the first tile becomes the session's own final-pick record: the same chooser applied to every completed fixture of the run. config/final_pick.tsv is written by final_pick.py --write, so both numbers derive from the instrument rather than from anything typed. First reading: baseline 82.1%, session 81.8% on 159 graded — the replay and the live run agree to within three tenths of a point
enginescripts/ingest_board.py ships — the board's completed matches now merge into the results bank (157 results into 28 leagues on first run). Resolver-guarded: a name that does not match the store is printed and skipped, never guessed (10 skipped, PSG/LAFC-style abbreviations); store.save deduplicates so re-running after each sweep is idempotent. As-of replay discipline is untouched — windows simply reach the current week now
boardEvery card now marks exactly ONE preferred lane — the ★ falls through the full protocol (weak/capped -> tip 3; playable tip 1; playable tip 2; any printed result lane; tip 1 as the engine's own pick). scripts/final_pick.py replays this exact chooser per league at n=300 against always-tip-1; verdict lands when the run finishes
engineBUY_REACH_CAP 0.03 ships — the bettor flagged tip 2 buy-from prices pulled absurdly low by the blend (−15% margin brackets on sub-60 team lanes). Measured before correcting: 8,404 team-lane offers land within two points of their claims in BOTH half-windows (sub-65: says 59.1 lands 59.2 / says 58.8 lands 57.0), so a 56% lane's fair price really is ~1.79 and the uncapped blend printing 1.47 reached 18% below reality. The blend now lifts the pricing probability at most 3 points above the lane's own number; its role above the record (asking extra of easy lanes) is untouched. 33 pending cards repriced
boardThe protocol's accent, on the card itself: a gold-rimmed '★ read first' lane. Strong-tier leagues (tip 1 baseline 85%+ on the replay) accent a playable tip 1; weak-tier (under 80%) and consensus-capped leagues accent tip 3 — the bettor's observation that the tiers invert between the two tips, made visible. Tiers derive from baselines.tsv, so accents move when the replay is re-run; settled cards drop the accent since it is reading guidance, not a grade. 31 lanes accented on today's board (24 tip 1, 7 tip 3)
boardProtocol step 2 lands in the layout: in a league whose tip 1 baseline runs under 80% (derived from baselines.tsv) or a consensus-capped one, a card whose tip 1 did not clear the playable bar now leads with tip 3 — the bettor's observation that the top lane should be the one worth reading first, the same principle as the playable tab's lead swap. Settled cards keep tip 1 order for consistent grading. Two cards flip on today's slate: Andorra-Eibar (capped L2) and Rennes-Le Mans (weak-tier Ligue 1)

The rule deliberately KEEPS the R6 softer unders bought a few cents under buy≥ (40 tickets, 87.5%, +8.3% ROI — push protection the claim never priced) and points result-lane money at the draw-covering shapes instead (12/X2 book: 7W 1L). The ledger of everything tried

Every feature suggestion and hypothesis put through the bar. Green cleared it, orange has not finished, red was rejected — and the number that rejected it is kept beside it.

Verified and helping36

Cleared two separate time windows and is live in the engine today.

09-01boardDoes Rule 6's safer neighbour apply to OVERS? (the bettor, off a Zürich v Young Boys card printing "TIP 1 O1.0 (rung O1.5)": "this needs to be normal O1.5. the U3.5 = U3.0 only applies there and with U4.25 = U4.5")
No, and the shipped rule had it backwards. The measurement it came from is entirely UNDERS — U3.0 against U3.5 at +2.86% versus +2.32%, U4.25 against U4.5 at -4.53% versus -5.34% — and the mechanism is specific: the better buy is the NO-PUSH line, because a push returns stake at 1.00 so the edge earns nothing on that portion, and with 18% of stake pushing 18% of the money sits out. For an under that means moving UP, off the push. But bought() was written as arithmetic rather than as the two cases measured — unders up a half, overs down a half — and for an over the printed line is ALREADY the no-push one: O1.5 loses on 0 or 1 and wins on 2+, while O1.0 returns stake on a one-goal game. So the shift moved TOWARD the push, the exact direction the measurement rejected. Two consequences, both live and both visible on today's board: 159 cards printing O1.5 were quoted as O1.0, where books offer about 1.01 because it is near-certain, so no over card could ever clear the 1.27 bar and the whole side was silently unplayable; and the 50 cards printing O0.5 were mapped to `O0.0`, which is not a bet at all. bought() is now the table it should always have been, STRUCK = {U3.0: U3.5, U4.25: U4.5}, with everything else bought as printed. Nothing in the original finding changes — both substitutions and both ROI comparisons stand exactly as recorded, because both are unders. On the first re-quote an O1.5 card played that could not have before: AGF v Midtjylland at 1.31 against the 1.27 bar. THE LESSON, which is the reusable part: a result measured on two lines was shipped as a rule about a DIRECTION, and the direction was inferred from symmetry rather than tested. Symmetry is not evidence. Enumerate what was measured
09-01boardShould a fixture that has already KICKED OFF be quoted and offered as a play? (not a hypothesis anyone proposed — found while re-fetching quotes after the name fold, when three of the board's four plays turned out to be matches already in progress)
No, and it was live. The odds feed keeps quoting a match after it starts, and those prices describe a game in progress rather than the one Athena forecast. Sheffield United v Bolton, 22 minutes played, quoted Under 4.5 at 1.83 where a pre-match line sits near 1.15 — long enough to clear the decline bar and print PLAY. Preston v Bristol City and Swansea v Watford were the same, all three kicked off at 18:45 UTC and re-quoted at 19:07. What makes it insidious is that all three were LEGITIMATE an hour earlier: the same card, the same engine, the same bar, and only the fetch time changed. The pre-match quote passes, the in-play quote passes by more, and nothing in the card says which one it is holding. Two guards, because one is not enough: odds_api.write_quotes now skips a started fixture, and webapp.verdict refuses to play one regardless of what the file says — the quote file outlives the moment it was written, so a render an hour later would go on offering a dead price. An unparseable kickoff returns False and is quoted as before, so a malformed row degrades rather than disappears. The board's playable set fell from four to one on the fix, and the one that survives is Luzern v Vaduz, tomorrow. Worth stating plainly: this had nothing to do with the name fold, it was simply the first time quotes were re-fetched mid-evening rather than in the morning, and every earlier session would have had the same exposure whenever a slate was re-quoted after kickoff
09-01engineShould every spelling of a club be folded onto one name, store-wide and ungated? (the bettor's call after the era-split merge surfaced the same defect at a hundred times the scale: "those names need to match smoothly. no more mistakes")
Shipped, and the honest verdict is that it is a CORRECTNESS fix rather than an accuracy one. The defect is real and was measured first: the results provider changed convention on 2024-08-10, terse trading names to full legal ones, and the store kept both, so 22.2% of every row stored since then was invisible to the spelling a hand-typed fixture resolves to — 64% in ESP-L2, 61% POR-PL, 47% ENG-PL — and Chelsea, Ajax, Porto, Roma, Juventus and Valencia each lost their entire modern record. Two layers now fold it in store.load_results: the canonical key (names.canonical, the resolver's own reduction, moved to app/data/names.py so one definition serves both instead of two copies drifting) for decoration, and 276 TYPED pairs for the case no algorithm should attempt, where one spelling abbreviates a word the other spells out — Man United against Manchester United, PSV against PSV Eindhoven, Inter against FC Internazionale Milano. 552 folds, 3 refusals. THE GUARD is a fact about football rather than a similarity threshold: a club plays at most one match a day, so two spellings sharing a date are different clubs and the fold is refused however well the names match, tested against everything already in a group so three names cannot join two clubs indirectly. All three refusals are correct (Paraguay's Club Nacional against Uruguay's, Finland's FC Inter against Inter Milan, a duplicated Hapoel row). NOW THE MEASUREMENT, which does not support an accuracy claim. Replaying 12,786 fixtures since 2024-08-10 twice, fold on and off, everything else identical: abstentions fall 496 to 317, so 179 fixtures are recovered and they grade 83.80%, right at the base rate. But on the 12,290 cards that print BOTH ways tip 1 goes 83.91% to 83.88% — nothing — and on the 263 where the fold CHANGES the rung it grades 83.65% against 82.13%, which is four cards out of 263 against a standard error of 2.32 points. Where the rung is unchanged the claim moves 0.06 points on average. So the replay says: neutral. WHY the replay cannot see the live defect, which matters more than the number: a replay looks each fixture up by its OWN stored row name, so a 2025 Chelsea match is read as `Chelsea FC` and gets the correct recent rows either way — by 2025 the long name already holds more than ROLLING_MATCHES=10. The live board is different: it types `Chelsea` by hand, which resolved to the 912-row spelling whose last match was May 2024. That is priced by the staleness study registered above (a window reaching four years back costs 1.47 points within-league) and by the board itself, where clubs read on stale rows fall from 114 to 12 and the twelve that remain are genuine — clubs that left the division. 13 pending cards re-priced, four changing market outright, all in ENG-CH. THREE FAILURES found while building it, each worse than the split it repaired: folding onto the name with most rows picks the DEAD spelling (Atlético de Madrid onto Ath Madrid, whose 456 rows all predate the change, so a board typing `Atlético Madrid` resolved to nothing — the primary is now the name the league most recently played under); typed pairs keyed by variant do not COMPOSE, so FRA-L1 stating both Brest = Stade Brestois and Stade Brestois = Stade Brestois 29 silently dropped Brest from its own club; and the fold can SHRINK what resolves, since `PSG` reached `Paris SG` through an alias whose membership guard then failed. Each is now a comment beside the code that fixes it. Green because the data defect is unambiguous and the recovered fixtures are free, NOT because the engine got more accurate — it did not, and claiming otherwise on four cards would be exactly the single-window reasoning this project has rejected six times
09-01engineShould a club whose history is split across two names by an ERA gap be merged, when the merge file had deliberately left it split? (FC Thun and FC Vaduz: both relegated and returned, so their deep history is filed under one name and their 2026 rows under another, leaving 3 rows against a 5-match gate and two abstentions on the 2 Sep slate)
Merged, after measuring the objection instead of accepting it. The file's own note said folding them would build a tip on six-year-old form — true, and now sized: across 68,341 replayed cards bucketed by how far back the rolling ten reaches, tip 1 grades 83.56% on a normal window (0-120 days), 82.17% at 730-1,460 days and 81.10% beyond four years. Raw that is -2.46 points, but the deep windows cluster in the cups and the Scottish lower divisions, which grade badly anyway; controlled WITHIN each league the drag is -1.47 points on an equal-league average over 35 leagues (-2.20 weighted, negative in 23 of 35). Real, one-directional and small — and the alternative is not a fresher tip but NO tip. The gate makes it temporary too: store._apply_merges only folds while the primary is under 5 rows, so the old form bridges the first weeks of a season and is then never read again. Checked first that no better source existed — SUI-CL holds two rows in total, both from a 2021 playoff, so the Swiss second tier simply is not stored. WHERE IT IS, a merge is the WRONG tool and must not be used: _apply_merges runs inside load_results, so a merged club clears min_matches and the cross-division fallback never fires — merging Coventry's 2000-01 Premier League rows onto its 2026 promotion would suppress a fallback that reads last season. Result on the board: both fixtures now price, SUI-SL's slice table was rebuilt (the league record gained 6 cards, FC Thun 84/100 became Thun 87/103), and Luzern v Vaduz came out orange at +5.63, STRONG, U4.5 at 1.41 against a 1.27 bar — the widest margin on the slate. Thun v Lausanne prices and correctly declines at O1.0 1.01. --reprice grew a --revive flag for this: an abstention is still an answer by default, but a pending abstention caused by a DATA defect since fixed was an answer to a different question
09-01engineShould a printed rung be struck at Rule 6's SAFER NEIGHBOUR or at the rung itself? (the bettor struck U3.5 where the card printed U3.0 and noticed Unibet pays 1.45 on U3.0 against 1.29 on U3.5 — the push line pays much more and returns the stake at three goals)
Safer neighbour, and for a reason worth keeping. First, the ROI tables were never confused: every script used lane=bought(mk) for BOTH the price and the settlement, which is correct because p_win('U3.0') equals p_win('U3.5') under the push-counts-as-hit convention while settle_fraction knows the difference (three goals pays 0.0 on U3.0, +1.0 on U3.5) — the hit-rate function priced and the money function settled. Then the comparison, both lines from the same fitted mu with the same margin and both settled on real results over 951 qualifying bets: bought line 1.35 odds, 75.0% win, ROI +0.86%; printed rung 1.49 odds, 57.0% win, 18.0% push, ROI +0.24%. The push line pays 10.5% more and returns less. By family U3.0->U3.5 gives +2.86% against +2.32% and U4.25->U4.5 gives -4.53% against -5.34%; applying the bar to the rung's own longer price is worse again at -1.53% on 1,967 bets, since the longer quote clears more often and admits weaker cards. THE MECHANISM: two forces oppose. The margin only bites the stake at risk, worth about P(push) x (1 - 1/overround) or roughly +0.95% for the push line; but a push returns stake at exactly 1.00 so the EDGE earns nothing on that portion, and with 18% pushing, 18% of the money sits out. With no edge the push line wins; with an edge you want more stake at risk, and the second force is larger by about 0.6 points. So the convention is right BECAUSE the rule has an edge — if the edge ever vanishes the better buy flips to the push line, which makes it a usable canary. Caveat per card: this is at derived prices, and Unibet's live board showed a 12.4% premium on U3.0 where the model implies 10.4%, so a book overpricing the push line by more than fair could favour it on that fixture — a line-shopping call, not a rule change
09-01boardShould measurement be restricted to the modern era? (the bettor's call: 2022-2026 is the game being played now, and a result that needs 2016 to hold is not a result about it)
Adopted, and it costs almost nothing — which is the informative part. Recomputing every registered label rate on 2022-2026 only moves the required prices by at most 0.007: super green 89.72% -> 89.75% (bar 1.181 unchanged), green 87.37 -> 87.68 (1.213 -> 1.209), orange 83.42 -> 83.35 (1.271 -> 1.272), red 77.88 -> 77.73 (1.361 -> 1.364), super red 77.38 -> 77.78 (1.370 -> 1.363). The tier ordering the five labels depend on holds in both cuts. The registered rates therefore STAY: they survive the restriction inside noise, and re-freezing thresholds to chase a third decimal would break the property that makes the forward log gradeable, namely that a card scored today faces the same bar as one scored last month. It also corrects how the two-window split was described: 55,544 of the bank's 62,528 cards are ALREADY 2022 or later and only 11% predates it, so calling the full-bank split 'nine years against two' was true of the date range and misleading about the data — card density rose sharply as leagues were added, so the older half running 2015 to May 2024 is itself about 78% post-2022. The split has effectively been 2022-2024 against 2024-2026 throughout, which is a healthier test than the labels implied. The priced ROI tables were never affected: they run 2024-07 to 2026-05, two consecutive seasons
09-01boardWhere does the decline rule's edge actually come from — the margin, or the price panel it is settled at? (asked while testing whether a looser bar could add playable lanes)
The panel, and it is not close. Every figure in the odds work is settled at the MAXIMUM across football-data's ~10 books; re-settling the IDENTICAL rule at Bet365 alone takes +1.71% to +0.62%, and at the market average to -0.69%. Same shape at every bar: +8% gives +2.08 / +1.15 / -0.31 and +4% gives +0.66 / -0.47 / -1.51. About 1.1 points of ROI separates best-of-market from one book at the same bar — more than the margin choice and more than the per-league question tested the same day — and at the market average the rule loses money at EVERY bar. Line shopping is not a refinement, it is most of the edge. Practical consequence for a bettor holding Dutch accounts only: four books is a real panel but the +1.71% is against ten including Pinnacle, unreachable from NL, so the honest expectation sits between the columns. From one live board, Unibet showed 1.08 where Pinnacle paid 1.12 and 1.44 where GTbets paid 1.49. Recorded alongside: the margin sweep in UNITS WON rather than ROI shows a plateau from +5% to +10% (units +14 to +22, both windows positive) collapsing below +4% (-9.6 units at +0%), so loosening the bar to buy volume trades a working rule for negative-expectation bets; and letting RED cards back in adds nothing, +9.4 units against +17.3 without, with red alone at -6.60% on 119 bets
08-30engineIs the DNB lane itself broken? (the book's 0W 4P 4L asked it)
Deep-dived on the 15,048-fixture result-lane dump with the shipped tilt and gates replayed: DNB is the BEST-calibrated thing tip 3 does — DNB1 says 73.2, wins 77.4 of decisive (+4.2) on 3,833 prints; DNB2 +11.7; every claimed band underclaims, monotonically (+1.7 at says 65-70 up to +8.7 at 85-90); pushes 23%. The live session agrees (18W 10P 8L, 69% of decisive). The book's 0-for-11 is the ticket SHAPE, not the prediction: three unpriced own-reads, short odds under the printed buy-from, and a push rate double the expected 23% in an 11-ticket sample. Rule 7 already carries the fix. One flag for later: the 12 lane is tip 3's only OVERclaimer (says 75.3, lands 73.3 on 7,884 prints) — worth a look before any 12-heavy strategy leans on it
08-29engineTeam-lane side attribution fails on regime breaks — the bettor's hypothesis from the Yukun/Forest cards: recent staff and squad churn shows up as a form streak the engine mistakes for quality
Measured on 8,404 replayed team-lane offers across 52 leagues (scripts/team_attrib.py): ALIGNED divergence — the last-8 scoring stub vs the long record, signed toward the rung — is monotone with overclaim: deflated +3.3, stable +1.2, mild inflation −1.7, strong (0.8+) −3.2 with both half-windows negative (−3.9/−2.5). The proposed data inputs measured differently: DEPTH of record (thin history) showed no consistent effect and shipped nothing, and league tables would have argued FOR the Yukun pick ("chasing the top"). STREAK_FROM 0.35 / STREAK_SLOPE 0.05 ships on the published number; treated region closes to +0.4/−1.0 per half; ~2.7% of offers stop printing. One-sided as always — deflated reads keep their honest underclaim. Honest limit: the debit fixes the population, not every anecdote — Yukun's own offer drew only 1.9 points because its edge came mostly from Zhejiang's base rate
08-28engineThe Playable-hit column's collapse leagues — the bettor spotted IRL-PD 83.2 falling to 68.6 on 35 lanes, CRO-1L and MEX-LMX similar
Confirmed and diagnosed on a 29,763-tip current-engine replay (retrosim --dump). Board-wide the playable layer is honest — says 82.3, hit 82.1, gap −0.1 on 15,813 lanes — and no playable-bar height beats +1% (raising it LOWERS hit in both halves), so the bar stands. The collapse is seven specific leagues (CRO-1L, MEX-LMX, IRL-PD, MAR-BP, COL-PA, ESP-L2, ITA-SA) whose positive-edge lanes overclaim >4 points in BOTH half-windows — pooled −6.4/−7.5 — flat across edge size, while their consensus lanes underclaim +3.4: there, disagreeing with the league consensus IS the model's error. The implied proportional debit (k≈2.0/2.3) empties their playable set anyway, so the honest ship is the CONSENSUS CAP: published probability capped at the market's league-baseline chance, edge can never exceed zero, no lane there ever badges playable. Board playable hit rises 82.1 → 82.6 (82.4→82.9 / 81.8→82.3 per half). Selection untouched, as always
08-28engineA tip claiming far more than its league's proven base rate is bragging — the bettor's hypothesis, confirmed one-sided
Measured on 32,493 replayed tips against each league's own hit rate: within ±2 points the claims are honest (gap −0.1); past +2 every extra claimed point delivers ~−1.2 back (slope −1.27/−1.04, both windows), and the wide-high band ran 5.6 hot in BOTH halves — while wide-LOW tips deliver their claims or better, so only one side is diseased. Filtering would lift playable hit just 86.4 → 86.7 (the braggarts still hit ~84); the real leak was the CLAIM, so REL_SAYS_DEBIT ships instead: 1.1 × the excess above league+2, published number only, selection raw. Every band closes to ±1.1 in both windows
08-27boardProof in hindsight: the losing era was volume, not the engine's ceiling
The pre-calibration weekend — 69.9% across 73 bets taken 'just going with it', ROI −10.1% — replayed through today's engine grades 84.2% taking every Tip 1 (avg buy≥ 1.30) and 83.3% at the playable bar. On the first calibrated slate the +1% bar filters 63 lanes to 42 and hits 95.0%: selection keeps the best winners. In-sample caveat attached — these weeks are the windows the constants were tuned on, so this is a ceiling estimate; the live confirmed number (84.6% on tips published before kickoff) landing in the same neighbourhood is the corroboration
08-27engineThe weakest leagues can be lifted the way the cups were — but only under an ROI constraint
Raised floors climb selection to safer rungs and lift hitrate in both windows everywhere — but the first pass measured only hitrate, and GRE-SL's 80.9 → 91.7 turned out to cost 66% → 6% of PLAYABLE volume (buy≥ 1.37 → 1.19): safe rungs nobody can buy edge on. Final rule: highest floor keeping ≥2/3 of playable volume. Six leagues ship 0.82, one 0.80, seven 0.78; GRE-SL and PER-L1 revert; MLS (worse at any floor) and ITA-SB (nothing to climb to) never shipped
08-27engineAbove says 90% the domestic board is too sure of itself
Measured on 32,493 tips across 45 leagues: below 90% the gap is −0.2 (honest); at 90%+ it is −1.2 ± 0.3, holds in both windows (−1.0 / −1.5) and is STRONGER in the played population (−1.8 / −1.4). Flat inside the band, so HIGH_SAYS_DEBIT 0.012 ships on the published number — selection stays raw
08-27boardPer-league gaps at n=200 are mostly noise wearing a number

Unfinished23

09-01engineWhen does the engine TRULY mean U3.0 rather than U3.5? (the bettor's rule: "U3.0 = U3.5 UNLESS truly U3.0")
Never, under the current convention — and finding out why exposed something larger. winning_totals() asks the GRADER which totals win a rung, and evaluate_market reports a whole line landing on its own number as half_win so the hit-rate column can count the push, which is the project's documented convention. The consequence nobody had traced: U3.0, U3.25, U3.5 and U3.75 ALL win on totals 0-3, so they carry identical p_win, identical edge, and are one bet to the selector. The same collapse hits O1.5/O1.75 and O2.25/O2.5/O2.75. score_markets breaks ties on (-edge, -p_win), both of which are equal here, so what actually decides which of four rungs the card prints is THE ORDER OF THE LADDER TUPLE — the exact thing that function's own comment says must not settle ties ("Ties are the normal case, not an edge case, and must not be settled by the order the ladder happens to be written in"). U3.0 wins because the string sits before U3.5 in LADDER. So the U3.0 -> U3.5 substitution is not swapping one bet for another; it is giving the bet the engine chose its honest name, the one whose win condition matches the arithmetic behind the printed claim. SHIPPED: bought() now substitutes only while same_bet(rung, sub) holds, asked of the engine's own winning_totals rather than asserted, so the "unless truly U3.0" clause is enforced instead of remembered — if a future change ever makes p_win tell the two apart, the substitution stops by itself. The same test is why the over side was wrong: O1.5 wins on 2+, O1.0 on 1+, different bets, and the old arithmetic swapped one for the other. NOT FIXED, and the reason it is orange: making the engine able to mean U3.0 requires p_win to stop counting the push as a win, which splits the selection convention from the hit-rate convention and re-prices every card in the bank. That invalidates every registered baseline, every calibration and every ROI table in docs/, so it is a project, not a patch. Worth noting the prize if it is ever done: four rungs currently indistinguishable would become four real choices, and the selector would be picking among them on evidence instead of on tuple order
09-01engineHow widespread is the split-name defect the merge file patches club by club? (asked because the scan for era splits was run store-wide rather than on the two clubs that surfaced)
Far wider than the merge file can reach, and it is a RECENCY defect wearing a naming costume. The results provider changed convention on 2024-08-10 — short trading names became full legal ones — and the store kept both, so most clubs in the major leagues now hold two or three spellings: Chelsea 912 rows to 2024-05-19 alongside Chelsea FC 76 rows covering the last two seasons; Man United under Man United, Manchester United AND Manchester United FC. Grouping every name in every league by a normalised key and asking the REAL resolver which spelling a hand-typed fixture lands on, 8,329 of the 37,556 rows stored since Aug 2024 — 22.2% — are invisible to the name that wins. ESP-L2 64.2%, POR-PL 60.7%, ENG-PL 47.4%, ESP-LL 44.7%, ENG-CH 31.3%. Ajax, Porto, Roma, Juventus, Valencia, Getafe and Chelsea each lose 70-77 recent rows, i.e. their entire modern record. This is NOT what team_merges fixes: that gate fires only when the primary is under 5 rows, and Chelsea has 912, so the merge mechanism is structurally unable to see it. It is also not currently costing the board much — 1 of 37 pending cards reads stale rows (Yokohama FM, resolved onto Yokohama FC, the exact failure the aliases file header warns about) — because ingest_board keeps feeding SHORT names back in, which keeps the short spelling roughly current for any club that has already appeared. Chelsea has not, so the first Chelsea card will price on spring-2024 form. NOT FIXED, deliberately: unconditional canonicalisation rewrites club history in 27 leagues and therefore every number measured on them, which is a call for the bettor rather than a tidy-up. The staleness measurement above prices the damage at roughly 1.5 points of hit rate per affected card
09-01boardIs the SYNTHETIC draw-no-bet price behind the +6.05% actually reachable? (it was built from the best home and best away quote across all books, assuming both takeable at once and carrying no DNB-market margin — if a real quote is 6% worse the edge is gone)
PASSES, at about +3.7%. Needs no history and no settlement, only today's board: fetch draw_no_bet and h2h for the same upcoming fixture in one request and compare. 60 credits, 24 fixtures, 48 comparable prices. Every single real quote is worse than the synthetic — no book gives the arbitrage away — but the shading is NOT flat with price: books shade hardest on longshots (2.60+ band, mean -6.07%, worst -16.30%) and barely at all on short favourites (1.00-1.25 band, mean -1.81%, worst -2.38%). The gated DNBs average 1.160, at the tight end. In the band actually bet (1.00-1.45) the mean gap is -2.50% and the median -2.34%, which takes the measured +6.04% to +3.66% / +3.81% rather than killing it. Break-even sits at P=1.086, a gap of -6.3%, and NONE of the 15 short-price quotes were worse than that. Two constraints the probe also found: draw-no-bet is quoted by only 1 to 4 books per fixture against 7 to 13 for the match odds, which caps stake rather than price; and NED-ED and POR-PL carry no DNB market at all, 6 of 30 fixtures probed, so a gated DNB there cannot be played as one. Residual uncertainty stated rather than hidden: the backtest's synthetic came from football-data MAX CLOSING while this probe's came from odds-api EU books pre-match, so if the former runs higher the true haircut is larger than -2.50% and this estimate is optimistic by that difference. The headroom absorbs a fair amount of it, but the number to trust is the forward log — n is 48 prices on one day against a 212-bet base result
09-01boardCan the guard decline on the PRICE rather than on the card? (the bettor's question: pick = tip X, but odds not worthy, so DECLINE — each label carries a measured hit rate, so it implies a break-even of 1/hit)
The first positive return in the whole odds line of work, and it is mostly one lane. Bars learned on the OLDER half and applied to the newer, which is cleanly out-of-sample by accident of coverage — football-data holds two seasons against the bank's four, so all 8,121 priced cards sit in the newer half and none touched the bars judging them. The gradient runs monotonically from -1.67% playing everything to -0.21% at break-even+4%, +0.83% at +6%, +1.49% at +8% and +1.59% at +12%, crossing zero around +5% — while the HIT RATE falls the whole way, 81.8% to 69.8%. That is the point: every earlier rule selected for hit rate and watched the quote shorten by exactly as much, whereas this one selects for cards the market has not shortened ENOUGH, and it is the first rule here hunting a book error rather than an engine strength. No single cell is significant (+/-1.77 on +0.83); the gradient across seven thresholds is the evidence. Two controls survived. The label beats a plain price bar at matched selectivity by 1.2-1.3 points at the tighter bars (+0.83 vs -0.41 at +6%, +1.49 vs +0.18 at +8%), and nothing at +4%. And it is NOT a fitted-mu artifact: selecting on a derived price while settling at the same derived price would bias returns upward where extrapolation is worst, but the effect is +1.48% in the moderate band (0.6-1.1 from the 2.5 line) and -4.53% in the heavy one (1.6+), the opposite signature. HOWEVER the ladder alone only reaches break-even: -1.88% playing everything to +0.03% at +6% and +0.69% at +8%, inside its error bars. The DNB lane carries it — 212 bets at +6.05% +/-1.98 with NO price bar, rising to +15.92% on the 57 kept at +6%, on prices that are exact rather than fitted (arithmetic from the book's 1X2, no Poisson step) and splitting +6.14% older against +5.96% newer. The market implies 86.2% of decisive there and Athena wins 92.0%, which is the same underclaim found on 30 Aug now showing up as money. 65 of the 90 kept super-green cards are these DNBs. Orange for one reason: the DNB price is SYNTHESISED from the best home and best away quote across all books, which assumes both are takeable at once and carries no DNB-market margin of its own, so a real single-book draw-no-bet quote will be worse. Settling that against real quoted draw_no_bet prices now outranks the team-total probe — tip 2's signal is larger in hit-rate terms but has never shown a positive return at any price, and this just did
09-01boardCan a card be scored by running it back through the board's OWN searches — the league's record on that lane, then filtered to each club, to the tip's side, and to club-and-side — counting each slice above or below the league baseline as a flag for or against, then pivoting or declining when the sum is bad? (the bettor's rule, worked by hand on Aston Villa v Arsenal 31 Aug, which printed O1.5 82.0% and finished 0-1)
Real, and almost entirely a restatement of the claim the card already prints. scripts/confluence.py builds every slice STRICTLY AS-OF in one forward pass — the search bar looks backwards over completed matches, so a slice it shows today contains the fixture you would have bet on, and scored that way the rule cannot fail. Over 25,494 graded cards in 57 leagues the continuous score is monotone across all ten deciles (80.62% at the bottom to 87.39% at the top) and keeping the top 40% grades 86.06% against an 83.65% base, older 86.04 newer 86.07. The hand-worked example reproduces exactly: score -2, matching the bettor's own count, with every slice within a few points of their board reading. But the ablation kills the novel part: the claim-vs-baseline check ALONE lifts +3.81 while all six searches together lift only +1.98, clubs-only lifts +0.18, and adding the club slices HALVES the claim signal. Head-to-head at matched selectivity the printed claim beats the score at every cut (+3.79 vs +2.40 keeping the top 40%), and where they disagree the claim is right — score-keeps-claim-drops grades 83.20% (below base) against claim-keeps-score-drops at 86.17%. Held fixed inside claim deciles the residual is +1.34 points, +2.14 older and +0.90 newer. Three further no's: inside the guard's RED tier the score INVERTS (79.4% at <=-2 against 76.2% at >=+2), so it can demote but never promote; no pivot beats standing the condemned pick (stand 82.22%, tip 3 78.17% even where one exists, tip 2 68.29%), so only NONE is better; and priced on 5,981 rungs at real closing odds the ROI goes -1.86% to -1.51% with the whole gain in the older window (-1.25 to -0.15) while the newer one worsens (-2.47 to -2.84). Depth does NOT help — beyond ten cards of club history the effect is flat at +2.4 to +3.3 and the sharpest separation is in the THINNEST bucket (+6.90) where the club slices cannot vote at all; the staleness explanation for that was tested and rejected, since windowing the slices is worse at every length (cumulative +2.17 against last-8 +1.85 and last-60 +1.96). CORRECTED the same day on two counts. First, that ablation table was NOT selectivity-matched — the claim row keeps 25.7% of cards and the six-search row 41.8%, and a stricter rule buys hit rate for free, so the pairing did not support the weight put on it; the six-search line is a real result on its own terms. Second, matched properly, the score IS additive: blending claim rank with score rank optimises at w=0.2-0.5 rather than 0, and inside the claim's own top 40% the score sorts the survivors monotonically across quartiles in both windows. The six searches also double-count — in a league that leans one way "Pisa" and "Pisa on unders" are the same thirty cards — and making the crossing report a residual against the club's own rate instead of the league removes the overlap, holding the signal while cutting its cross-window gap from 1.38 points to 0.09. Then the bank was DOUBLED to 62,528 graded cards (1,500 fixtures per league, ~4 seasons) to settle the bettor's tank question directly, and the effect roughly HALVED: the quartile spread inside the claim's top 40% falls from +3.57 to +2.08 and the blend's gain from +0.10 to +0.07 at keep-40%. Everything survives in sign, nothing in size — more data shrank the finding rather than confirming it, which is the honest answer to whether filling the tank helps. Priced on 8,121 final picks at real closing odds: everything -1.67%, claim top 40% -1.57% at 86.2% hit, blended top 15% -0.96% (n=245, +/-2.03, a lead not a result), and claim-top-40-best-quartile -2.44% — WORSE than the claim alone in both windows at the same hit rate, because the extra selectivity picks shorter prices than it picks winners. Selection 1 is the whole project in one line: 4.4 points of hit rate bought 0.1 points of ROI while the average quote fell 1.16 to 1.13. RE-CHECKED once more and two claims fell. The red-tier INVERSION does not exist: it came from ~420 and ~1,630 cards on the shallow bank with the sign score, and on 62,528 cards with the de-duplicated continuous score red sorts the normal way at +2.29, positive in both windows. Promotion is still wrong, but for a different reason — red's BEST score quartile grades 79.27% against orange's WORST at 81.64%, so red's ceiling sits under orange's floor, which is a claim about levels the data supports rather than about direction, which it does not. And the era instability (+2.14 older / +0.90 newer shallow, against +1.48 / +3.04 deep) was the wrong cut entirely: it is not league mix, whose shares barely move between halves, it is REGION. The effect is European — +3.57 quartile spread in Europe with both windows positive, EXACTLY ZERO in the Americas at -0.06 (and -0.41 in one half), and RoW at +4.27 on 5,929 cards swinging +5.77 to +1.75, too thin to read. So the finding is not 'a card can be scored against its own searches' but 'a card in a European league can be'. That also kills the blended top-15% selection as a lead: it is 23.8% RoW against 9.5% of the bank, two and a half times over-represented in exactly the region where the score does not work, so its -0.96% on 245 cards is a noisy sub-population landing favourably rather than a signal. Club memory swept properly — windowing ONLY the club reads, leaving league and side cumulative — is NEUTRAL, every setting from last-20 to last-730-days within 0.15 points; last-40 ships as a reasoned default because it never loses and squads really do change, not as a measured gain. That sweep also explains an earlier wrong conclusion: the previous windowing test cut the league-side slice too, a statistic built from hundreds of cards with no staleness problem, which is why it showed harm. Orange because the residual is worth a fourth, small, ONE-DIRECTIONAL input to the existing guard, applied to EUROPEAN leagues only, sized for +2 to +3.5 points — and not a five-colour system, not a reroute rule, and not a reason to enlarge the bank
09-01boardCan a green/orange/red risk guard, read off Athena's own card before any result is known, flag the plays that go wrong? (green = a gated DNB or tip 1 claiming >=84% on an edge under 1.0; red = tip 1 under 76%, or under 80% on an OVER; orange = the rest)
It separates HITRATE reliably and ROI only in one window. Graded on 7,673 final picks settled at football-data's maximum closing price across 16 European leagues — result lanes derived exactly from 1X2, ladder rungs through a mu fitted to the 2.5 line — the whole book returns -1.70% (82.1% at 1.15). By tier: green 87.0% hit / -1.05%, orange 82.5% / -1.08%, red 75.8% / -4.30%. Dropping red lifts the book to -1.07%, so the answer to 'will red take out most of the bad ones' is yes on strike rate. But the two-window split refuses the money claim: older half red -1.17% against green -0.91% and orange -0.88% — worth nothing — while the newer half runs red -6.28%. The hitrate gap holds in BOTH halves (86.7 vs 77.2 older, 87.3 vs 74.9 newer), so what red really marks is a lower strike rate that the market sometimes prices correctly and sometimes does not. Independent support from the thin-league run the same day: green -2.47% against orange -4.40% on 836 picks. Ships as a CONFIDENCE colour on the card, not as a profitability filter — -1.07% is still a loss, and half the evidence for the lift is one time period
09-01engineAre the goal-expectation ratings better built from SHOTS ON TARGET than from goals? (the store already holds hst/ast; goals are the noisy realisation and shots regress better between matches)
Measured and promising, not yet in the engine. scripts/shot_ratings.py replays both models as-of in one forward pass with an identical functional form, window, shrinkage and Poisson scoring — only the statistic differs — and scores them on U1.5/U2.5/U3.5/U4.5, four lines neither model selects. Run over ALL 128,532 fixtures the store holds. Shots ALONE are not reliable — -1.09% overall, and -2.37% in the older half, which is what patchy shot recording in the thinner leagues and older seasons looks like. But a MIXTURE beats goals alone and does it in both windows independently: w=0.4 on shots gives +0.96% of Brier overall, +0.80% in the older half (which prefers w=0.3) and +1.24% in the newer (which prefers w=0.5). The optimum is a flat basin from 0.3 to 0.5, not a spike, which is what a real effect looks like rather than a fitted one. On the 8 richest leagues alone the same sweep gives +1.07% at w=0.6. For scale, the whole gap to the bookmaker's closing line is 2.3%, so this is roughly half of it. CAVEAT that governs what happens next: the baseline here is a standalone goals model scoring 0.1925, while the real engine scores 0.1876 — it is already better than the thing shots beat, so the gain cannot be assumed to transfer. The next step is the shots signal inside the real pipeline, re-measured the same way. One bug found and fixed mid-experiment: an all-time cumulative conversion rate penalised only the shots model and reversed the whole result, turning +0.35% into -3.01%
09-01boardStar tip 3 only when it is a DNB claiming more than 2 points above tip 1 — otherwise always star tip 1 (fell out of testing the profile chooser above)
Validated and not yet shipped. The shipped chooser is measurably behind always taking tip 1: 82.09% against 83.64% on the 17,995-card walk-forward, and 82.08% against 83.49% on the project's own tables (final_pick.tsv against baselines.tsv, 57 leagues, picker worse in 36 of them). It drops to tip 3 whenever tip 1 is not playable, and tip 3's baseline sits five points below tip 1's, so it trades down by construction. The gate fixes the trade: on the 426 cards where a DNB out-claims tip 1 by more than 2 points, the DNB grades 94.13% against tip 1's 82.16%, +11.97. It holds with DNB pushes stripped out entirely (+9.58 on 355 cards), so it is not a settlement artefact, and it clears BOTH windows independently (+7.44 older, +16.59 newer; +5.79 and +13.94 with pushes removed). It also holds whether or not tip 1 is playable (+11.38 playable, +13.86 not), so it needs no playable exception. DOUBLE CHANCE must be excluded: at the same gate a DC switch LOSES 3.53 points. Whole-board effect is small because it fires on 1.42% of cards (+0.170 points over always-tip-1) — the real gain is the 1.7 points from retiring the current rule
08-30boardThe star restricted to tip 1 or tip 3 (the bettor's follow-up to the final-picking table)
Scored offline from the upgraded dump — the instrument now stores every lane's grade per fixture, so a variant costs a query instead of a replay. Dropping tip 2 recovers most of the loss: 79.5% -> 82.1% league-average, against always-tip-1's 83.5%. The residue is one honest swap: on the 3,715 fixtures with no playable tip 1 but a printed result lane, tip 3 lands 77.9% where tip 1 lands 84.3%. On HITRATE the star should never leave tip 1. On MONEY it can — the swap pays if the result lane is priced 8%+ above the total, which the buy>= bracket already answers per card. Orange because the money side is unmeasured: no odds history exists to settle it, and the live book is the only instrument that can
08-29engineTip 3 — the result lane (1X/X2/12, sharpening to DNB when one side dominates), the bettor's proposal, SHIPPED ON PROBATION
Measured first on 15,048 fixtures across 52 leagues (scripts/result_lanes.py): the per-side expectations through two Poissons price the result market honestly once the home side is tilted 1.10 (raw home wins ran +3.2 hot in both halves; tilted, all six outcome gaps close within 1.4). The feared Poisson draw bias never appears — TEAM_SHRINK compresses the sides and pumps the draw by roughly what Poisson drops it. The strongest-DC band that supplies three quarters of prints grades +0.1/−0.5; every stronger band and every DNB band UNDERCLAIMS (+1.3 to +8.5) — the lane's failure mode is modesty. Outside every tally and the hero until live results earn it in, exactly the cup lane's probation path
08-29engineA push should count as a win at the ROOT, not just in the tallies — the bettor's rule: the standing offset plays U3.0 as U3.5, so the actual play wins where the printed rung pushes
The board's counters, playable tallies and hero window now grade ◦ as a hit (shipped 29 Aug). The deeper change — hit_weight("push")=1.0, which would fold push totals into winning_totals and hence into the PUBLISHED probabilities and selection ties — is a real engine change: U3.0 would collapse into the U3.25/U3.5 tier, tips would reshuffle, and every baseline (retrosim table, REL bases, floors) needs re-derivation on two windows before it ships. Until then the claims exclude the push and the tally credits it, which overstates hit vs says by the push rate (~1-2 points on U3.0-heavy lanes) — known, accepted, and queued

Capped lanes all land sub-bar where nothing is bought, and REL rarely bites hard on board lanes — small, but the gate run backwards should someday reverse everything the gate applies
08-28ledgerThe widened staking strategy — taking lanes under buy≥ when probability still clears

The played-lane record historically grades 1–2 points ABOVE its stated probability, which covers most of that shortfall — but not with a margin.
